=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a circular cake divided into N pieces by cut lines. Each cut line is a line segment connecting the center of the circle to a point on the arc.
The pieces and cut lines are numbered 1, 2, \ldots, N in clockwise order, and piece i has a mass of A_i. Piece 1 is also called piece N + 1.
Cut line i is between pieces i and i + 1, and they are arranged clockwise in this order: piece 1, cut line 1, piece 2, cut line 2, \ldots, piece N, cut line N.
We want to divide this cake among K people under the following conditions. Let w_i be the sum of the masses of the pieces received by the i-th person.

- Each person receives one or more consecutive pieces.
- There are no pieces that no one receives.
- Under the above two conditions, \min(w_1, w_2, \ldots, w_K) is maximized.

Find the value of \min(w_1, w_2, \ldots, w_K) in a division that satisfies the conditions, and the number of cut lines that are never cut in the divisions that satisfy the conditions. Here, cut line i is considered cut if pieces i and i + 1 are given to different people.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Let x be the value of \min(w_1, w_2, \ldots, w_K) in a division that satisfies the conditions, and y be the number of cut lines that are never cut. Print x and y in this order, separated by a space.

Constraints


- 2 \leq K \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 10^4
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 2
3 6 8 6 4

Sample Output 1

13 1

The following divisions satisfy the conditions:

- Give pieces 2, 3 to one person and pieces 4, 5, 1 to the other. Pieces 2, 3 have a total mass of 14, and pieces 4, 5, 1 have a total mass of 13.
- Give pieces 3, 4 to one person and pieces 5, 1, 2 to the other. Pieces 3, 4 have a total mass of 14, and pieces 5, 1, 2 have a total mass of 13.

The value of \min(w_1, w_2) in divisions satisfying the conditions is 13, and there is one cut line that is not cut in either division: cut line 5.

Sample Input 2

6 3
4 7 11 3 9 2

Sample Output 2

11 1

Sample Input 3

10 3
2 9 8 1 7 9 1 3 5 8

Sample Output 3

17 4

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:
def build_next(T):
    nxt = [0]*N
    add = [0]*N
    j = 0
    pref_local = pref
    N_local = N
    for i in range(N):
        if j < i+1:
            j = i+1
        limit = i + N  # allow full circle
        target = pref_local[i] + T
        while j <= limit and pref_local[j] < target:
            j += 1
        # j <= limit guaranteed if S>=T
        if j < N_local:
            nxt[i] = j
            add[i] = 0
        else:
            nxt[i] = j - N_local
            add[i] = 1
    return nxt, add
But wait, if j = i+N, then j-N = i, add=1. Good.

However, if T is high and for some i j reaches i+N, for next i, j remains previous i+N. New limit = i+N = previous i+N+1? Let's compute i increases by1, limit increases by1, so j = old limit = new limit -1, okay. The while may advance. Good.

Now, we need be absolutely sure that y = count of infeasible starts at x. Let's prove more rigorously for final explanation? Final answer only code, but we need ensure.

Proof:
For any optimal division, take any cut line c used. The K segments starting from c and going clockwise form a valid sequence of K gaps each sum >=x. Let f be minimal next cut with gap sum >=x. Since f(c) <= next cut in the division, by induction f^K(c) <= c+N. Thus c is a feasible start (greedy K-cycle from c is valid). Conversely, if f^K(c) <= c+N, the greedy cuts c, f(c), ..., f^{K-1}(c) form a valid division with K segments each sum >=x, hence optimal. Therefore the set of cut lines that are cut in at least one optimal division equals {c | f^K(c) <= c+N}. So y is N minus size of that set. Wait "never cut" = not in any optimal division = not feasible start. Yes.

Need ensure "f^K(c) <= c+N" in actual cut positions, not residue condition. Our residue check c==0 or c==1 and r<=i exactly tests actual position <= i+N. Let's verify:
Let res_r = r, res_c = c. Actual position = r + c*N.
Condition actual <= i+N.
If c=0: r <= i+N. Since r<N and i+N>=N, true.
If c=1: r+N <= i+N => r<=i.
If c>=2: r+2N <= i+N => r+N <= i, impossible since r>=0,N>0. So false.
Thus okay.

Now, one more subtlety: The greedy K-cycle from c uses cuts f^t(c) for t=0..K-1. If f^K(c) < c+N, the last segment from f^{K-1}(c) to c+N has sum >= sum from f^{K-1}(c) to f^K(c) because extra pieces positive. But what if f^K(c) = c+N? Last segment exactly sum>=T. Good. If f^K(c) > c+N, no valid K-cycle starting at c. Good.

Now, f(i) definition: minimal j > i with sum(i..j-1) >= T. If f(i)=i+N (full circle), then greedy K-cycle for K>=2 invalid because first segment all pieces; but f^K(i)>i+N so not counted. In residue model, if f(i)=i+N, add=1, next=i. For K=2, actual f^2(i)=i+2N > i+N. In binary lifting, res_c after K jumps will be K? Let's see if next[i]=i, add[i]=1. For K jumps, residue remains i, c=K. For K>=2, c>=2, infeasible. Good.

But in building f, if f(i)=i+N, then f(i) is not a valid cut for a segment because it's the same cut after full circle. But it's okay. However, for f(i) to be minimal j with sum>=T, if j=i+N, then sum=S. Since T<=S/K<=S, exists. Good.

For a state (r,c), applying f once gives (nxt[r], c+add[r]). Because actual position = r + cN; f(actual) = f(r) + cN = (nxt[r] + add[r]*N) + cN = nxt[r] + (c+add[r])N. Yes.

Because after first 2^b jumps from i, residue = pow_r[i], wraps = pow_c[i]. Then second 2^b jumps from that residue add pow_c[pow_r[i]] wraps. Good.

new_res_c[i] = min(2, res_c[i] + pow_c[res_r[i]])
Good.

Let's write jump_all:

def jump_all(T):
    # build nxt, add
    nxt = [0] * N
    add = [0] * N
    pref_local = pref
    N_local = N
    j = 0
    for i in range(N_local):
        if j < i + 1:
            j = i + 1
        limit = i + N_local
        target = pref_local[i] + T
        # while loop
        while j <= limit and pref_local[j] < target:
            j += 1
        if j < N_local:
            nxt[i] = j
            # add[i]=0
        else:
            nxt[i] = j - N_local
            add[i] = 1

    pow_r = nxt
    pow_c = add
    res_r = list(range(N_local))
    res_c = [0] * N_local
    LOG = K.bit_length()
    for b in range(LOG):
        if (K >> b) & 1:
            old_res_r = res_r
            # new_res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
            # new_res_c = [2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N)]
            # But after assigning res_r, res_c comprehension uses old_res_r and old res_c. We can compute both before assigning.
            new_res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
            new_res_c = [0] * N_local
            # To avoid enumerate? Use for loop? Let's use comprehension with range and old_res_r.
            new_res_c = [2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N_local)]
            res_r = new_res_r
            res_c = new_res_c
        # update pow
        old_pow_r = pow_r
        # new_pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
        new_pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
        new_pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N_local)]
        pow_r = new_pow_r
        pow_c = new_pow_c
    return res_r, res_c

But note: In res update, `new_res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]` uses old res_r. Then `new_res_c` uses `res_c[i]` and `old_res_r[i]`. `res_c` is still old. Good. But `pow_c[old_res_r[i]]` uses old res_r. Good.

Maybe there is an O(N logN) algorithm for max-min K-subset on circle using "binary search on answer" but check O(N) with "two pointers and queue" not logK. Let's revisit.

We have points on a circle with coordinates pref[0..N-1] modulo S. Need select K points with spacing >=T. The greedy algorithm from a point selects the next point at distance >=T. The maximum number of selected points starting from any point can be found by "doubling" but maybe there is a linear-time algorithm for "maximum cardinality T-separated subset on a circle" using "sliding window" because the points are sorted and T is fixed.

Consider the complement: We want to choose as many points as possible such that consecutive selected points are at least T apart. This is equivalent to finding the maximum number of points you can "hit" with intervals of length T? Actually if you select a point, the next selected point must be at least T away. The greedy from a start picks the earliest possible next point. The maximum number of selected points on a circle is the maximum over starts of greedy count. There is a known algorithm: duplicate points, compute `next[i]` (first point at distance >=T). Then the maximum number of points in a T-separated subset is the maximum number of jumps before exceeding i+S. This can be computed by "binary lifting" or by "two pointers" if we process starts in order and maintain a queue of jump counts? Let's try to derive.

For each start i, define `cnt[i]` = number of jumps until > i+S. We want max cnt. There is a recurrence:
cnt[i] = 1 + cnt[next[i]]? Not exactly because cnt[next[i]] uses window next[i]+S, not i+S. But since next[i] > i, the remaining distance to i+S is S - (next[i]-i). We need count jumps starting from next[i] within a smaller window. Define `dp[i]` = maximum number of jumps starting at i before exceeding i+S. Then dp[i] = 1 + max t such that f^t(next[i]) <= i+S. This is not dp[next[i]] because dp[next[i]] allows up to next[i]+S, which is larger. However, maybe we can compute dp[i] by "sliding" if we know the position after dp[next[i]] jumps? Let p = f^{dp[next[i]]}(next[i]) <= next[i]+S. We need <= i+S. Since i+S < next[i]+S, p may be too large. So dp[i] <= dp[next[i]]. Could be dp[next[i]] or dp[next[i]]-1 etc. Since f jumps are at least 1, maybe dp[i] differs by at most 1? Let's test sample T=11: dp? c array [1,1,2,1,1,2]. next = [3,3,5,2,2,2]? Actually f residues: f0=3, f1=3, f2=5, f3=2 (8-6), f4=2, f5=2. dp[0]=1, dp[next[0]=3]=1 equal. dp[1]=1, dp[3]=1. dp[2]=2, next[2]=5, dp[5]=2 equal. dp[3]=1, next[3]=2, dp[2]=2 (diff +1). So dp[i] can be dp[next[i]] or dp[next[i]]-1? dp[3]=1 < dp[2]=2. So not simple.

We have f on doubled positions. For each start i, the sequence of positions p_t = f^t(i). We stop when p_t > i+N. This is equivalent to: For each position p, consider all starts i such that p is reachable and p <= i+N. The maximum t for i is the number of reachable positions along its orbit before deadline. Maybe use "offline dynamic programming" with a Fenwick tree? For each i, f^K(i) is the K-th ancestor. Level ancestor in a tree can be answered in O(1) with O(N) preprocessing using Euler tour and ladder decomposition. Since our graph is a forest of paths (parent > child), we can build a tree and use a linear-time level ancestor algorithm. But implementing that in Python for each check is too much. However, maybe we can precompute the tree once for each T? No.

But note K is fixed across binary search. We need K-th ancestor for all nodes in a forest where parent = f_T. There is a known "level ancestor in O(1) with O(N)" but complex. Binary lifting is simpler.

Maybe we can use "jump pointers with block decomposition" to answer K-th ancestor in O(sqrtN) per query, but we need all N queries per check -> O(N sqrtN) worse.

Could use "binary lifting with bitset of wraps" but still log.

Now, one more subtlety: The greedy cycle from c might have fewer than K distinct cuts if f^t(c) repeats? Since f(i)>i actual, f^t(c) strictly increasing until maybe >c+N. For K jumps and f^K(c)<=c+N, all f^t(c) for t=0..K-1 are < f^K(c) <= c+N, so distinct. Good.

Now, let's decide final code structure.

We'll write:
import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,K = map(int,input().split())
    A = list(map(int,input().split()))
    S = sum(A)
    pref = [0]*(2*N+1)
    for i in range(2*N):
        pref[i+1] = pref[i] + A[i % N]

    LOG = K.bit_length()
    N_local = N
    pref_local = pref
    K_local = K

    def build_next(T):
        nxt = [0]*N_local
        add = [0]*N_local
        j = 0
        pref_l = pref_local
        Nl = N_local
        for i in range(Nl):
            if j < i + 1:
                j = i + 1
            limit = i + Nl
            target = pref_l[i] + T
            # while j <= limit and pref_l[j] < target
            while j <= limit and pref_l[j] < target:
                j += 1
            if j < Nl:
                nxt[i] = j
            else:
                nxt[i] = j - Nl
                add[i] = 1
        return nxt, add

    def jump_all(T):
        nxt, add = build_next(T)
        pow_r = nxt
        pow_c = add
        res_r = list(range(N_local))
        res_c = [0]*N_local
        for b in range(LOG):
            if (K_local >> b) & 1:
                old_res_r = res_r
                res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
                # Need res_c uses old_res_r and old res_c
                # But res_c is still old; pow_c old.
                res_c = [2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N_local)]
            if b == LOG - 1:
                break
            old_pow_r = pow_r
            pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
            pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N_local)]
        return res_r, res_c

Wait, in res_c comprehension, `res_c[i]` uses old res_c? Yes, because res_c hasn't been reassigned yet? Actually we assign `res_r` first, then `res_c = [...]`. `res_c` on RHS refers to old list. Good. But `res_r` has been reassigned; we use `old_res_r` for index. Good.

In pow_c comprehension, `pow_c[i]` old; `old_pow_r[i]` old. Good.

But there is a bug: In `res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]`, if `pow_r` is old. Good. Then `res_c` comprehension uses `pow_c[old_res_r[i]]`; old_res_r is old res_r. Good.

However, if we update `res_r` before `res_c`, and then in `res_c` comprehension we use `res_c[i]` old, but `res_c` is still old. Good.

Now, check(T):
    res_r, res_c = jump_all(T)
    for i in range(N_local):
        c = res_c[i]
        if c == 0:
            return True
        if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
            return True
    return False

But jump_all always computes full res. For binary search, we can implement a separate `check` with early false to save time. Let's decide.

Maybe we can modify jump_all to accept `early=False`. If early True, use for loop for res update and return None if alive==0; at end return res. But final y needs full. Let's implement check with early to improve.

However, code complexity and potential bugs. Let's write a separate `check(T)` that uses binary lifting but with early false and maybe list comprehensions for pow.

Pseudo:
def check(T):
    nxt, add = build_next(T)
    pow_r = nxt
    pow_c = add
    res_r = list(range(N))
    res_c = [0]*N
    alive = N  # number with c<=1? Initially all.
    for b in range(LOG):
        if (K >> b) & 1:
            new_res_r = [0]*N
            new_res_c = [0]*N
            alive = 0
            # local vars for speed
            pr = pow_r; pc = pow_c; rr = res_r; rc = res_c
            for i in range(N):
                r = rr[i]
                nr = pr[r]
                nc = rc[i] + pc[r]
                if nc > 2:
                    nc = 2
                new_res_r[i] = nr
                new_res_c[i] = nc
                if nc <= 1:
                    alive += 1
            res_r = new_res_r
            res_c = new_res_c
            if alive == 0:
                return False
        if b == LOG - 1:
            break
        old_pow_r = pow_r
        pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
        pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N)]
    # after all bits, scan alive? We have alive count of c<=1, but need r<=i for c==1.
    for i in range(N):
        c = res_c[i]
        if c == 0:
            return True
        if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
            return True
    return False

But note `alive` after res update counts c<=1. If alive==0 return False. If alive>0, continue. At the end, scan. Good.

Maybe similar. For early false, for loop can save pow updates. Let's keep for loop for check.

Now, let's revisit the proof that every cut in an optimal division is a feasible start. We used f^K(c) <= c+N. But f is minimal next cut with sum >=x. Conversely, if c feasible, greedy cuts c, f(c), ..., f^{K-1}(c) are K cuts. Need ensure the last segment from f^{K-1}(c) to c+N has sum >=x. Since f^K(c) <= c+N, and f^K(c) is the end of the K-th greedy segment from f^{K-1}(c). The sum from f^{K-1}(c) to f^K(c) >=x. If f^K(c) < c+N, then the segment to c+N contains that greedy segment plus extra positive pieces, so sum >=x. Thus valid. Good.

Now, let's consider if `check` uses `N_local` from outer scope; in Python nested function variable lookup is slower. We can bind local variables inside functions. We already do in build_next. In check, use local Nl = N, Kl = K, LOG local. Good.

Let's write check with local bindings:

```
    def check(T):
        nxt, add = build_next(T)
        Nl = N
        Kl = K
        LOG = K.bit_length()
        pow_r = nxt
        pow_c = add
        res_r = list(range(Nl))
        res_c = [0] * Nl
        for b in range(LOG):
            if (Kl >> b) & 1:
                pr = pow_r
                pc = pow_c
                rr = res_r
                rc = res_c
                new_r = [0] * Nl
                new_c = [0] * Nl
                alive = 0
                for i in range(Nl):
                    r = rr[i]
                    nr = pr[r]
                    nc = rc[i] + pc[r]
                    if nc > 2:
                        nc = 2
                    new_r[i] = nr
                    new_c[i] = nc
                    if nc <= 1:
                        alive += 1
                res_r = new_r
                res_c = new_c
                if alive == 0:
                    return False
            if b == LOG - 1:
                break
            old_pow_r = pow_r
            pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
            pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(Nl)]
        for i in range(Nl):
            c = res_c[i]
            if c == 0:
                return True
            if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
                return True
        return False
```

Note: `LOG = K.bit_length()` computed each check; we can compute once outside and pass. Since K fixed, define LOG_GLOBAL = K.bit_length() outside and use in functions.         nxt, add = build_next(T)
        Nl = N
        Kl = K
        LOG = K.bit_length()
        pow_r = nxt
        pow_c = add

                old_res_r = res_r
                res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
                res_c = [2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(Nl)]
            if b == LOG - 1:
                break
            old_pow_r = pow_r
            pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
            pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(Nl)]
        return res_r, res_c
```

Let's think of an O(N) check again, maybe possible with "two-pointers and queue" for this specific problem. Since we have time, let's try to derive.

We need know if exists i with f^K(i) <= i+N. This is equivalent to the maximum number of T-separated points on a circle. There is a known linear-time algorithm for maximum cardinality subset with minimum distance T on a circle:
- Compute `next[i]` as before.
- Use "doubling" but maybe we can compute the maximum number by "two pointers" on `next` because `next` is monotone. Let's search memory: For points on a circle, to find max number of points with distance at least T, you can start at any point and greedily select. The maximum over starts can be found by considering the point with the smallest next? Actually, if you start at a point that is not selected in some optimal solution, you can shift? There is a property: There exists an optimal greedy selection starting from any point that is selected? Not.

For a set of points on a circle, the maximum size of a T-separated subset can be found by:
1. Duplicate points.
2. Compute `nxt[i]`.
3. Use binary lifting or "two-pointer" to compute for each i the number of points selected before exceeding i+C. But maybe because `nxt` is monotone, the function `cnt[i]` can be computed by maintaining a deque of selected points as i increases.

Let's attempt to compute `cnt[i]` (max jumps before > i+N) for all i in O(N). Define `p = i`, `cnt=0`. As i increases by 1, how does the greedy sequence change? The first selected point `f(i)` is nondecreasing. The subsequent selected points are also nondecreasing. The stopping condition is position > i+N. Since both start and threshold increase by 1, maybe the number of selected points changes by at most 1? Let's test sample T=11 c=[1,1,2,1,1,2]. Differences: 0,+1,-1,0,+1. It changes by at most1. Is that always true? If so, we can maintain c(i) by sliding and update by checking? Let's analyze.

Let p_t(i)=f^t(i). For i+1, p_t(i+1) >= p_t(i). The stopping threshold increases by1. The count c(i) = max t with p_t(i) <= i+N. For i+1, threshold i+1+N. Since p_t(i+1) >= p_t(i), count could decrease if p_t jumps a lot, or increase if threshold increases and p_t doesn't jump. The difference could be more than1? Suppose p_K(i) <= i+N but p_{K+1}(i)>i+N. For i+1, p_K(i+1) could be much larger than i+1+N, causing count drop many? But p_K(i+1) - p_K(i) could be large, while threshold increased by1, so count could drop by many if p_K jumps by >? However, c(i) counts up to K; could drop by more than1. Is p_t(i+1)-p_t(i) bounded by? f(i+1)-f(i) can be large, so p_K difference large. Example maybe c drops from5 to1. So not simple.

But maybe we only need max c(i), and can use a greedy "start at 0, if count<K, move start to next point"? There is an algorithm for "maximum points in interval length L" with monotone next: To find if there exists a chain of K jumps within length N, we can simulate from i=0 and if it fails, move i to f(0)? For a fixed start i, greedy selects K points. If it fails (f^K(i)>i+N), then any start before f(i)? Maybe also fails? We want find if any start works. Could use a "sliding window" of K-th ancestor? Hmm.

Suppose we start at i=0. If f^K(0) <= N, feasible. If not, then f^K(0) > N. Consider starting at i=1. The first selected point f(1) >= f(0). The K-th selected f^K(1) >= f^K(0)? Since f^K nondecreasing, yes f^K(1) >= f^K(0) > N. But threshold for i=1 is N+1. It could still be feasible if f^K(1) <= N+1. Since f^K(1) could equal f^K(0) (plateau) or N+1. If f^K(0) = N+1, then start0 failed (N+1 > N), start1 feasible (N+1 <= N+1). So moving start by1 can fix if f^K plateaus. This suggests we can scan i and maintain f^K(i) with a pointer? Since f^K nondecreasing, and threshold increases by1, feasibility condition f^K(i) - i <= N. Define h(i)=f^K(i)-i. h can decrease by at most? f^K(i+1)-f^K(i) can be 0, so h can decrease by1. It can increase by large. Thus h can cross threshold multiple times. To find any h<=N, we might need check all i. But maybe h never increases after it decreases? Not sure. Our constructed feasible set non-contiguous shows h can go above and below. So need all.

Could compute h(i) using "sliding window" if we can update f^K(i) incrementally. f^K(i+1) can be found from f^K(i) by advancing? Since f^K nondecreasing, f^K(i+1) >= f^K(i). We could start at f^K(i) and move forward until it's the K-th ancestor of i+1? Not trivial.

Let's implement `compute_res(T, early=False)`:
```
    def compute_res(T, early=False):
        nxt, add = build_next(T)
        ...
        for b in range(LOG):
            if (K >> b) & 1:
                old_res_r = res_r
                res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
                res_c = [2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N)]
                if early:
                    # if all wraps >=2, no chance
                    all_dead = True
                    for c in res_c:
                        if c <= 1:
                            all_dead = False
                            break
                    if all_dead:
                        return None
            if b == LOG - 1:
                break
            old_pow_r = pow_r
            pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
            pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(N)]
        return res_r, res_c
```
The all_dead loop can be written as `if all(c >= 2 for c in res_c): return None`. This stops early if alive found. Use generator.

But note: If early and all_dead after some bit, return None. If K has no more set bits? Still infeasible. Good.

For final y, call `compute_res(x, False)`.

Now, check:
```
    def check(T):
        res = compute_res(T, True)
        if res is None:
            return False
        res_r, res_c = res
        for i in range(N):
            c = res_c[i]
            if c == 0:
                return True
            if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
                return True
        return False
```

This is clean.

Before final, let's review code for syntax.

Full code draft:

```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:]
    del data
    S = sum(A)
    minA = min(A)
    maxA = max(A)
    A2 = A * 2
    pref = [0] * (2 * N + 1)
    for i in range(2 * N):
        pref[i+1] = pref[i] + A2[i]
    del A, A2

    LOG = K.bit_length()
    Nl = N
    pref_l = pref

    def build_next(T):
        nxt = [0] * Nl
        add = [0] * Nl
        j = 0
        for i in range(Nl):
            if j < i + 1:
                j = i + 1
            limit = i + Nl
            target = pref_l[i] + T
            while j <= limit and pref_l[j] < target:
                j += 1
            if j <= limit:
                if j < Nl:
                    nxt[i] = j
                else:
                    nxt[i] = j - Nl
                    add[i] = 1
            else:
                nxt[i] = i
                add[i] = 1
        return nxt, add

    def compute_res(T, early=False):
        nxt, add = build_next(T)
        pow_r = nxt
        pow_c = add
        res_r = list(range(Nl))
        res_c = [0] * Nl
        for b in range(LOG):
            if (K >> b) & 1:
                old_res_r = res_r
                res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
                res_c = [2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(Nl)]
                if early and all(c >= 2 for c in res_c):
                    return None
            if b == LOG - 1:
                break
            old_pow_r = pow_r
            pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
            pow_c = [2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s for i in range(Nl)]
        return res_r, res_c

    def check(T):
        res = compute_res(T, True)
        if res is None:
            return False
        res_r, res_c = res
        for i in range(Nl):
            c = res_c[i]
            if c == 0:
                return True
            if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
                return True
        return False

    low = minA
    high = min(S // K, (S - maxA) // (K - 1)) + 1
    if high <= low:
        high = low + 1
    while high - low > 1:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        if check(mid):
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid
    x = low

    res_r, res_c = compute_res(x, False)
    y = 0
    for i in range(Nl):
        c = res_c[i]
        if c == 0:
            continue
        if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
            continue
        y += 1
    print(x, y)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Need check `pref_l = pref` outside; in build_next, `pref_l` is local? It's from outer, but we assign `pref_l = pref` in main, then build_next uses it as free variable. That's fine. But inside build_next, `pref_l` is not assigned, so it's a free variable; lookup slightly slower. We can set `pref_l = pref` inside build_next for local speed:
```
    def build_next(T):
        pref_l = pref
        Nl = N
        ...
```
But `Nl` is also free. We can do:
```
    def build_next(T):
        nxt = [0] * Nl
        add = [0] * Nl
        p = pref_l
        ...
```
Since `pref_l` is local to main but free in build_next, Python uses cell variable, slower. To make local, at start of build_next:
`p = pref_l`
Then use p. But `pref_l` itself is a cell. Accessing cell once per function call is okay. Inside loop use p local. Let's modify:
```
    def build_next(T):
        nxt = [0] * Nl
        add = [0] * Nl
        p = pref_l
        j = 0
        for i in range(Nl):
            ...
            target = p[i] + T
            while j <= limit and p[j] < target:
```
Good.

Similarly, in compute_res, `Nl`, `K`, `LOG` are free; we can bind:
```
    def compute_res(T, early=False):
        nxt, add = build_next(T)
        Nl_local = Nl
        K_local = K
        LOG_local = LOG
        ...
```
But `Nl` is a cell? Actually Nl is a local variable in main, referenced in nested functions, so it's a cell. Accessing cell in loop slower. We can bind to local at start:
```
        Nl_local = Nl
        Kl = K
        LOG_local = LOG
```
Then use `Nl_local` in loops. Let's do that for speed.

In check, bind `Nl_local = Nl`.

=== ANSWER ===
I'll provide the final Python code.

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:]
    del data

    S = sum(A)
    minA = min(A)
    maxA = max(A)

    A2 = A * 2
    pref = [0] * (2 * N + 1)
    for i in range(2 * N):
        pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + A2[i]
    del A, A2

    LOG = K.bit_length()
    Nl = N
    pref_l = pref

    def build_next(T):
        nxt = [0] * Nl
        add = [0] * Nl
        p = pref_l
        j = 0
        for i in range(Nl):
            if j < i + 1:
                j = i + 1
            limit = i + Nl
            target = p[i] + T
            while j <= limit and p[j] < target:
                j += 1
            if j <= limit:
                if j < Nl:
                    nxt[i] = j
                else:
                    nxt[i] = j - Nl
                    add[i] = 1
            else:
                nxt[i] = i
                add[i] = 1
        return nxt, add

    def compute_res(T, early=False):
        nxt, add = build_next(T)
        Nl_local = Nl
        Kl = K
        LOG_local = LOG

        pow_r = nxt
        pow_c = add
        res_r = list(range(Nl_local))
        res_c = [0] * Nl_local

        for b in range(LOG_local):
            if (Kl >> b) & 1:
                old_res_r = res_r
                res_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_res_r]
                res_c = [
                    2 if (s := res_c[i] + pow_c[old_res_r[i]]) > 2 else s
                    for i in range(Nl_local)
                ]
                if early and all(c >= 2 for c in res_c):
                    return None

            if b == LOG_local - 1:
                break

            old_pow_r = pow_r
            pow_r = [pow_r[r] for r in old_pow_r]
            pow_c = [
                2 if (s := pow_c[i] + pow_c[old_pow_r[i]]) > 2 else s
                for i in range(Nl_local)
            ]

        return res_r, res_c

    def check(T):
        res = compute_res(T, True)
        if res is None:
            return False
        res_r, res_c = res
        for i in range(Nl):
            c = res_c[i]
            if c == 0:
                return True
            if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
                return True
        return False

    low = minA
    high = min(S // K, (S - maxA) // (K - 1)) + 1
    if high <= low:
        high = low + 1

    while high - low > 1:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        if check(mid):
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid

    x = low

    res_r, res_c = compute_res(x, False)
    y = 0
    for i in range(Nl):
        c = res_c[i]
        if c == 0:
            continue
        if c == 1 and res_r[i] <= i:
            continue
        y += 1

    print(x, y)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
